## GHSA-f25v-x6vr-962g · pheditor/pheditor

2026-07-24 · PHP · **CRITICAL** 10.0 · Pattern: `MISSING_AUTH→ENDPOINT`

### Root cause

The vulnerability existed because the application had a hardcoded default password 'admin' which, when set, triggered a forced password change flow. During this flow, the application did not verify the current password provided by the user against the actual stored password. Instead, it only checked if the submitted password was 'admin' (which was hardcoded into a hidden input field in the password change form), allowing an attacker to bypass authentication and set a new password without knowing the original one.

### Impact

An attacker could completely bypass the authentication mechanism, gain administrative access to the Pheditor application, and potentially execute arbitrary code or modify files on the server, leading to full system compromise.

### Key diff

```
--- a/pheditor.php
+++ b/pheditor.php
@@ -152,7 +152,9 @@
 
 if (empty(PASSWORD) === false && (isset($_SESSION['pheditor_admin'], $_SESSION['pheditor_password']) === false || $_SESSION['pheditor_admin'] !== true || $_SESSION['pheditor_password'] != PASSWORD)) {
     if (isset($_POST['pheditor_password']) && empty($_POST['pheditor_password']) === false) {
-        if (PASSWORD == hash('sha512', 'admin')) {
+        $submitted_hash = hash('sha512', $_POST['pheditor_password']);
+
+        if (PASSWORD == hash('sha512', 'admin') && $submitted_hash === PASSWORD) {
             if (isset($_POST['pheditor_new_password']) && isset($_POST['pheditor_confirm_password'])) {
                 if ($_POST['pheditor_new_password'] === 'admin') {
                     $error = 'Password cannot be admin';
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a check to ensure that when the hardcoded 'admin' password triggers a forced password change, the submitted password hash also matches the actual stored password. This prevents an attacker from simply submitting 'admin' as the current password without knowing the real password, thereby enforcing proper authentication during the password change process.

## GHSA-w28w-gp39-m4p6 · @prompty/core

2026-07-24 · JavaScript · **CRITICAL** 10.0 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→TEMPLATE`

### Root cause

The Nunjucks templating engine was used to render user-controlled templates and inputs without sufficient sanitization or sandboxing. This allowed attackers to access and invoke dangerous properties and methods (like `__proto__`, `constructor`, `prototype`) through template expressions, leading to arbitrary code execution.

### Impact

An attacker could achieve remote code execution on the server by injecting malicious template code, potentially compromising the entire system.

### Key diff

```
--- a/runtime/typescript/packages/core/src/renderers/nunjucks.ts
+++ b/runtime/typescript/packages/core/src/renderers/nunjucks.ts
@@ -13,11 +13,91 @@ import type { Prompty } from "../model/agent/prompty.js";
 import type { Renderer } from "../core/interfaces.js";
 import { prepareRenderInputs } from "./common.js";
 
+type NunjucksRuntime = {
+  memberLookup: (object: unknown, property: unknown) => unknown;
+  callWrap: (callable: unknown, name: string, context: unknown, args: unknown[]) => unknown;
+};
+
+const UNSAFE_PROPERTIES = new Set(["__proto__", "constructor", "prototype"]);
+
 const env = new nunjucks.Environment(null, {
   autoescape: false,
   throwOnUndefined: false,
 });
 
+function safeMemberLookup(object: unknown, property: unknown): unknown {
+  if (typeof property === "string" && UNSAFE_PROPERTIES.has(property)) {
+    throw new Error(`Unsafe template member access: ${property}`);
+  }
+
+  if (
+    (typeof property !== "string" && typeof property !== "number") ||
+    object === null ||
+    typeof object !== "object"
+  ) {
+    return undefined;
+  }
+
+  const descriptor = Object.getOwnPropertyDescriptor(object, property);
+  return descriptor !== undefined && "value" in descriptor ? descriptor.value : undefined;
+}
+
+function safeCallWrap(_callable: unknown, name: string, _context: unknown, _args: unknown[]): never {
+  throw new Error(`Template function calls are not allowed: ${name}`);
+}
+
+function sanitizeValue(value: unknown, seen = new WeakMap<object, unknown>()): unknown {
+  if (value === null || typeof value === "string" || typeof value === "number" || typeof value === "boolean") {
+    return value;
+  }
+
+  if (typeof value !== "object") {
+    return undefined;
+  }
+
+  const existing = seen.get(value);
+  if (existing !== undefined) {
+    return existing;
+  }
+
+  if (Array.isArray(value)) {
+    const result: unknown[] = [];
+    seen.set(value, result);
+    for (const item of value) {
+      result.push(sanitizeValue(item, seen));
+    }
+    return result;
+  }
+
+  const result = Object.create(null) as Record<string, unknown>;
+  seen.set(value, result);
+  for (const [key, descriptor] of Object.entries(Object.getOwnPropertyDescriptors(value))) {
+    if (!UNSAFE_PROPERTIES.has(key) && "value" in descriptor) {
+      result[key] = sanitizeValue(descriptor.value, seen);
+    }
+  }
+  return result;
+}
+
+function sanitizeInputs(inputs: Record<string, unknown>): Record<string, unknown> {
+  return sanitizeValue(inputs) as Record<string, unknown>;
+}
+
+function renderSafely(template: string, inputs: Record<string, unknown>): string {
+  const runtime = nunjucks.runtime as unknown as NunjucksRuntime;
+  const memberLookup = runtime.memberLookup;
+  const callWrap = runtime.callWrap;
+  runtime.memberLookup = safeMemberLookup;
+  runtime.callWrap = safeCallWrap;
+
+  try {
+    return env.renderString(template, inputs);
+  } finally {
+    runtime.memberLookup = memberLookup;
+    runtime.callWrap = callWrap;
+  }
+}
+
 export class NunjucksRenderer implements Renderer {
   async render(
     agent: Prompty,
     template: string,
     inputs: Record<string, unknown>,
   ): Promise<string> {
     const [modified] = prepareRenderInputs(agent, inputs);
-    return env.renderString(template, modified);
+    return renderSafely(template, sanitizeInputs(modified));
   }
 }
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces `safeMemberLookup` and `safeCallWrap` functions to restrict access to unsafe properties and prevent function calls within templates. It also includes `sanitizeValue` and `sanitizeInputs` to recursively clean input data by creating a new object with only safe properties, effectively sandboxing the template rendering environment.

> → GHSA-gg2g-p7xc-qqmm
> → GHSA-65p8-9433-jpcp

## GHSA-rjg6-39jm-rgg4 · @better-auth/scim

2026-07-24 · JavaScript · **CRITICAL** 9.9 · Pattern: `MISSING_AUTHZ→RESOURCE`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from the SCIM provider's update functionality not properly validating email uniqueness during user updates (PUT/PATCH operations). An attacker could change a user's email to one already registered by another user, leading to a collision. Additionally, the system did not properly handle user deactivation via the 'active' SCIM attribute, failing to revoke sessions or enforce the deactivation consistently.

### Impact

An attacker could take over another user's account by reassigning their email address. They could also maintain access to a deactivated account if their sessions were not properly revoked, or bypass deactivation entirely if the 'admin' plugin was not present.

### Key diff

```
--- a/packages/scim/src/routes.ts
+++ b/packages/scim/src/routes.ts
@@ -850,19 +932,37 @@ export const updateSCIMUser = (authMiddleware: AuthMiddleware) =>
 				});
 			}
 
+			const email = getUserPrimaryEmail(
+				body.userName,
+				body.emails,
+			).toLowerCase();
+			const name = getUserFullName(email, body.name);
+			const emailChanged = email !== user.email;
+
+			if (emailChanged) {
+				await assertSCIMEmailAvailable(ctx, email, userId);
+			}
+
+			const userUpdate: Record<string, unknown> = {
+				email,
+				name,
+				updatedAt: new Date(),
+			};
+			if (emailChanged) {
+				// A reassigned email is unverified until the new address is confirmed.
+				userUpdate.emailVerified = false;
+			}
+			if (body.active !== undefined) {
+				userUpdate.banned = body.active === false;
+			}
+			const deactivating = resolveSCIMActiveDeactivation(ctx, userUpdate);
+
 			const [updatedUser, updatedAccount] =
 				await ctx.context.adapter.transaction<[User | null, Account | null]>(
 					async () => {
-						const email = getUserPrimaryEmail(body.userName, body.emails);
-						const name = getUserFullName(email, body.name);
-
 						const updatedUser = await ctx.context.internalAdapter.updateUser(
 							userId,
-							{
-								email,
-								name,
-								updatedAt: new Date(),
-							},
+							userUpdate,
 						);
 
 						const updatedAccount =
@@ -875,6 +975,10 @@ export const updateSCIMUser = (authMiddleware: AuthMiddleware) =>
 					},
 				);
 
+			if (deactivating) {
+				await ctx.context.internalAdapter.deleteUserSessions(userId);
+			}
+
 			const userResource = createUserResource(
 				ctx.context.baseURL,
 				updatedUser!,
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces `assertSCIMEmailAvailable` to enforce email uniqueness during user updates. It also adds `resolveSCIMActiveDeactivation` to correctly map SCIM `active` status to the internal `banned` field, revoke user sessions upon deactivation, and ensure the admin plugin is present for deactivation. The `deleteSCIMUser` function was also updated to only delete the global user if no other accounts are linked.

## GHSA-7gfh-x38p-prh3 · velocityjs

2026-07-24 · JavaScript · **CRITICAL** 9.8 · Pattern: `PROTOTYPE_POLLUTION→OVERRIDE`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from insufficient prototype chain protection in the Velocity.js template engine. An attacker could craft a template that, when evaluated, would traverse the prototype chain using keys like 'constructor' and 'prototype' to access and manipulate sensitive JavaScript built-in objects, specifically the Function constructor. This allowed for arbitrary code execution.

### Impact

An attacker could achieve arbitrary remote code execution within the context of the application running the Velocity.js template engine, leading to full system compromise or data exfiltration.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/compile/references.ts
+++ b/src/compile/references.ts
@@ -31,12 +32,12 @@ export class References extends Compile {
     const isSilent = this.silence || ast.leader === '$!';
     const isFunction = ast.args !== undefined;
     const context = this.context;
-    let ret = context[ast.id];
+    let ret = this.isBlockedPathKey(context, ast.id) ? undefined : context[ast.id];
     const local = this.getLocal(ast);
 
     const text = getRefText(ast);
 
-    if (text in context) {
+    if (hasOwnProperty(context, text)) {
       return ast.prue && escape ? convert(context[text]) : context[text];
     }
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a new `prototype-guard.ts` module with functions to explicitly block access to dangerous prototype chain keys like `__proto__`, `constructor`, and `prototype` when they are not own properties or when accessed on a function's prototype. These new guard functions are integrated into the `set.ts` and `references.ts` compilation logic to prevent malicious prototype chain traversal during template evaluation.

## GHSA-wg5r-wc3x-39vc · org.openidentityplatform.openam:openam-core

2026-07-24 · Java · **CRITICAL** 9.8 · Pattern: `DESERIALIZATION→RCE`

### Root cause

The application allowed unauthenticated attackers to control the class name passed to `Class.forName` and subsequently `newInstance()` without proper validation. Additionally, a separate deserialization vulnerability existed where an encrypted but attacker-controlled serialized Java object (Subject) could be deserialized without a class allowlist, leading to gadget-chain execution.

### Impact

An unauthenticated attacker could achieve remote code execution on the server by specifying a malicious class name or by crafting a malicious serialized object, leading to full compromise of the system.

### Key diff

```
--- a/openam-core/src/main/java/com/sun/identity/authentication/share/AuthXMLUtils.java
+++ b/openam-core/src/main/java/com/sun/identity/authentication/share/AuthXMLUtils.java
@@ -1609,7 +1611,19 @@ static DSAMECallbackInterface createCustomCallback(
             
             if (callback == null) {
                 if ((className != null) && (className.length() != 0)) {
-                    Class xmlClass = Class.forName(className);
+                    Class xmlClass = Class.forName(className, false,
+                        AuthXMLUtils.class.getClassLoader());
+                    if (!DSAMECallbackInterface.class.isAssignableFrom(xmlClass)) {
+                        debug.error("createCustomCallback : class " + className
+                            + " is not a DSAMECallbackInterface implementation");
+                        return null;
+                    }
                     callback = (DSAMECallbackInterface) xmlClass.newInstance();
                 }
             }
@@ -1784,6 +1836,7 @@ public static Subject getDeSerializedSubject(String subjectSerialized)
             //convert byte to object using streams
             byteIn = new ByteArrayInputStream(byteDecrypted);
             objInStream  = new ObjectInputStream(byteIn);
+            objInStream.setObjectInputFilter(SUBJECT_DESERIALISATION_FILTER);
             tempObject = objInStream.readObject();
```

### Fix summary

The patch modifies `Class.forName` to prevent static initializers from running and adds a check to ensure the loaded class implements `DSAMECallbackInterface` before instantiation. It also introduces an `ObjectInputFilter` for `Subject` deserialization, allowing only a predefined set of safe classes (primitives, `Principal` implementations, and specific JDK/security classes) to prevent arbitrary object deserialization.

## GHSA-p279-2cqp-84jg · org.openidentityplatform.opendj:opendj-server-legacy

2026-07-24 · Java · **CRITICAL** 9.6 · Pattern: `PRIVILEGE_ESCALATION→ROLE`

### Root cause

The OpenDJ SASL PLAIN mechanism handler did not properly enforce access control checks for authorization identities (authzId). Specifically, it failed to call the `checkProxyAccess` method, which verifies if the authenticated user has the necessary 'proxy' access rights to assume a different authorization identity. This omission allowed an authenticated user to bypass the intended ACI scope checks.

### Impact

An attacker could authenticate as a legitimate user and then assume the identity of another user, including privileged accounts, without proper authorization. This leads to privilege escalation, allowing the attacker to perform actions with the assumed user's permissions.

### Key diff

```
--- a/opendj-server-legacy/src/main/java/org/opends/server/extensions/PlainSASLMechanismHandler.java
+++ b/opendj-server-legacy/src/main/java/org/opends/server/extensions/PlainSASLMechanismHandler.java
@@ -330,6 +331,11 @@ public void processSASLBind(BindOperation bindOperation)
               return;
             }
           }
+
+          if (! checkProxyAccess(bindOperation, userEntry, authZEntry))
+          {
+            return;
+          }
         }
       }
       else
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces calls to a new `checkProxyAccess` method within the `processSASLBind` function of the `PlainSASLMechanismHandler`. This method delegates to `SASLContext.hasProxyAccess` to ensure that the authenticated user has the 'proxy' access control right for the target authorization identity, thus enforcing the ACI scope check.

## GHSA-68r5-9hpg-7qw9 · org.openidentityplatform.opendj:opendj-dsml-servlet

2026-07-24 · Java · **CRITICAL** 9.4 · Pattern: `SSRF→INTERNAL_ACCESS`

### Root cause

The application's DSMLv2 gateway allowed unauthenticated users to supply an xsd:anyURI value, which was then dereferenced (fetched) server-side without proper validation or resource limits. This meant an attacker could control the URL the server would connect to.

### Impact

An attacker could force the server to make requests to internal network resources (SSRF), read local files (e.g., file:// protocol), or cause a denial of service by making the server read an unbounded amount of data from a slow or malicious source.

### Key diff

```
--- a/opendj-dsml-servlet/src/main/java/org/opends/dsml/protocol/ByteStringUtility.java
+++ b/opendj-dsml-servlet/src/main/java/org/opends/dsml/protocol/ByteStringUtility.java
-      try (InputStream is = ((URI) obj).toURL().openStream())
-      {
-        ByteStringBuilder bsb = new ByteStringBuilder();
-        while (bsb.appendBytes(is, 2048) != -1)
-        {
-          // do nothing
-        }
-        return bsb.toByteString();
-      }
+      return convertUri((URI) obj);
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a secure-by-default mechanism to disable URI dereferencing. When enabled, it now enforces an allowlist for URI schemes (defaulting to http/https), filters out requests to loopback/link-local/private IP addresses, prevents HTTP redirects, and caps the maximum content length read from a URI.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 95 occurrences**

## GHSA-r277-6w6q-xmqw · github.com/getkin/kin-openapi

2026-07-24 · Go · **CRITICAL** 9.1 · Pattern: `INSECURE_DEFAULT→CONFIG`

### Root cause

The `ValidationHandler` in `kin-openapi` had a default `AuthenticationFunc` set to `NoopAuthenticationFunc` if not explicitly configured. This 'no-operation' function effectively bypassed any authentication checks by default, leading to a fail-open scenario.

### Impact

An attacker could bypass authentication for endpoints protected by `ValidationHandler` if the `AuthenticationFunc` was not explicitly set, gaining unauthorized access to protected resources.

### Key diff

```
--- a/openapi3filter/validation_handler.go
+++ b/openapi3filter/validation_handler.go
@@ -44,9 +44,7 @@ func (h *ValidationHandler) Load() error {
 	if h.Handler == nil {
 		h.Handler = http.DefaultServeMux
 	}
-	if h.AuthenticationFunc == nil {
-		h.AuthenticationFunc = NoopAuthenticationFunc
-	}
+	// NOTE: users MUST set AuthenticationFunc explicitly or expect ErrAuthenticationServiceMissing when verifying SecurityRequirements
 	if h.ErrorEncoder == nil {
 		h.ErrorEncoder = DefaultErrorEncoder
 	}
```

### Fix summary

The patch removes the default assignment of `NoopAuthenticationFunc` to `h.AuthenticationFunc`. Users are now required to explicitly set an `AuthenticationFunc`, and if not set, the system will return an `ErrAuthenticationServiceMissing` when verifying security requirements, preventing accidental authentication bypass.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 24 occurrences**

## GHSA-vh45-f885-3848 · sm-crypto

2026-07-24 · JavaScript · **CRITICAL** 9.1 · Pattern: `WEAK_CRYPTO→RANDOM`

### Root cause

The original implementation of the `sm-crypto` library, specifically for SM2 key generation in Node.js, relied on a default random number generator (RNG) that used `Math.random()` combined with the system's wall clock time (`new Date().getTime()`). This approach provides insufficient entropy, making the generated keys predictable.

### Impact

An attacker could potentially predict the private keys generated by the library. This would allow them to forge signatures, decrypt sensitive communications, or impersonate legitimate users, leading to a complete compromise of cryptographic security.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/sm2/utils.js
+++ b/src/sm2/utils.js
-const {BigInteger, SecureRandom} = require('jsbn')
+const {BigInteger} = require('jsbn')
 const {ECCurveFp} = require('./ec')
+const {SecureRandom} = require('./rng')
```

### Fix summary

The patch replaces the insecure `SecureRandom` implementation from `jsbn` with a custom `SecureRandom` class. This new implementation prioritizes `window.crypto.getRandomValues` (Web Crypto API) or `node:crypto.getRandomValues` for generating cryptographically strong random numbers, falling back to an Arcfour-based PRNG seeded with system time only if no strong crypto API is available. It also explicitly throws an error if `globalThis.crypto.getRandomValues` is not supported, preventing insecure key generation.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 4 occurrences**
> Languages: Java, PHP, Erlang, JavaScript
> → GHSA-cj8j-37rh-8475
> → GHSA-2528-jw5q-ww88
> → GHSA-v9gv-xp36-jgj8

## GHSA-7g7r-gx96-252g · app-builder-lib

2026-07-24 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 7.8 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→COMMAND`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from the `electron-updater` package using `execWine` to run the uninstaller without proper path sanitization or environment control. This allowed an attacker to manipulate the search path for executables, leading to the execution of a malicious binary instead of the legitimate uninstaller.

### Impact

An attacker could achieve arbitrary code execution by placing a malicious executable in a predictable location within the search path, which would then be executed with the privileges of the application during the uninstallation process.

### Key diff

```
--- a/packages/app-builder-lib/src/targets/nsis/NsisTarget.ts
+++ b/packages/app-builder-lib/src/targets/nsis/NsisTarget.ts
@@ -447,7 +438,8 @@ export class NsisTarget extends Target {
         }
       }
     } else {
-      await execWine(installerPath, null, [], { env: { __COMPAT_LAYER: "RunAsInvoker" } })
+      const wineVm = new WineVmManager(packager.config.toolsets?.wine)
+      await wineVm.exec(installerPath, [], { env: { __COMPAT_LAYER: "RunAsInvoker" } })
     }
     await packager.signIf(uninstallerPath)
```

### Fix summary

The patch replaces the direct use of `execWine` with `WineVmManager.exec`, which provides a more controlled and isolated environment for executing Wine commands. It also removes direct calls to `getPath7za` and `exec` in favor of encapsulated utility functions, reducing the risk of path manipulation.

## GHSA-4w2j-m93h-cj5j · quinn-proto

2026-07-24 · RUST · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The `quinn-proto` library, used for QUIC connections, did not properly limit the number of out-of-order stream frames that could be buffered for reassembly. An attacker could send many small, non-contiguous frames, causing the `Assembler` to store an unbounded number of `Buffer` chunks, leading to excessive memory consumption.

### Impact

An attacker could send specially crafted QUIC stream frames to a server or client using `quinn-proto`, causing it to consume an ever-increasing amount of memory. This would lead to a denial-of-service (DoS) condition as the application or system runs out of memory and crashes or becomes unresponsive.

### Key diff

```
--- a/quinn-proto/src/connection/assembler.rs
+++ b/quinn-proto/src/connection/assembler.rs
@@ -209,6 +214,10 @@ impl Assembler {
             self.defragment();
             // ngtcp2 uses a threshold of 4000 -- try to be a little more conservative?
             if self.data.len() > 1024 {
                 return Err(TooManyChunks);
             }
         }
+
         Ok(())
     }
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a new error type `TooManyChunks` and modifies the `insert` method in the `Assembler` to return a `Result`. After defragmentation, if the number of buffered data chunks (`self.data.len()`) exceeds a threshold (1024), an `Err(TooManyChunks)` is returned, preventing unbounded growth of the buffer.

## GHSA-j5g9-f88f-gfj3 · httplib2

2026-07-24 · Python · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The httplib2 library previously decompressed gzipped or deflated content without any limits on the output size. An attacker could send a specially crafted, small compressed payload that, when decompressed, expands into an extremely large amount of data, consuming excessive memory or CPU resources.

### Impact

An attacker could trigger a Denial of Service (DoS) condition on a system using httplib2 to process HTTP responses, leading to resource exhaustion, application crashes, or unresponsiveness.

### Key diff

```
--- a/httplib2/__init__.py
+++ b/httplib2/__init__.py
@@ -389,19 +393,15 @@
 
 
 def _decompressContent(response, new_content):
-    content = new_content
+def _decompressContent(response, new_content, limit_kwargs):
     try:
-        encoding = response.get("content-encoding", None)
-        if encoding in ["gzip", "deflate"]:
-            if encoding == "gzip":
-                content = gzip.GzipFile(fileobj=io.BytesIO(new_content)).read()
-            if encoding == "deflate":
-                try:
-                    content = zlib.decompress(content, zlib.MAX_WBITS)
-                except (IOError, zlib.error):
-                    content = zlib.decompress(content, -zlib.MAX_WBITS)
+        if encoding in ["gzip", "deflate", "zlib"]:
+            try:
+                content = limit_wrap(ZlibDecoder()).consume_bytes(new_content, 0)
+            except (IOError, zlib.error):
+                content = limit_wrap(DeflateDecoder()).consume_bytes(new_content, 0)
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a new `LimitDecoder` class and integrates it into the content decompression logic. This decoder enforces configurable limits on the output-to-input amplification ratio and the total output length, preventing unbounded decompression. New `decode_limit_*` parameters are added to the `Http` client to allow users to configure these limits.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 113 occurrences**

## GHSA-j6g5-3hh3-pgw8 · bedrock-agentcore

2026-07-24 · Python · **HIGH** 7.3 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→COMMAND`

### Root cause

The application constructed a shell command for 'pip install' by directly concatenating user-supplied package names without proper escaping. This allowed an attacker to inject arbitrary shell commands by crafting malicious package names that would break out of the intended argument and execute additional commands.

### Impact

An attacker could execute arbitrary commands on the underlying system where the bedrock-agentcore package is running, potentially leading to remote code execution and full system compromise.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/bedrock_agentcore/tools/code_interpreter_client.py
+++ b/src/bedrock_agentcore/tools/code_interpreter_client.py
@@ -606,7 +610,8 @@ def install_packages(
             if not VALID_PACKAGE_NAME.match(pkg):
                 raise ValueError(f"Invalid package name: {pkg}")
 
-        packages_str = " ".join(packages)
+        # Quote each argument so it is passed to the command as a single literal token.
+        packages_str = " ".join(shlex.quote(pkg) for pkg in packages)
         upgrade_flag = "--upgrade " if upgrade else ""
         command = f"pip install {upgrade_flag}{packages_str}"
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces `shlex.quote()` to properly escape each package name before it is included in the `pip install` command. It also tightens the regular expression for `VALID_PACKAGE_NAME` to restrict the characters allowed within package extra specifiers, further limiting potential injection vectors.

## GHSA-29w2-fq35-v728 · awslabs.aws-api-mcp-server

2026-07-24 · Python · **HIGH** 7.0 · Pattern: `INSECURE_DEFAULT→CONFIG`

### Root cause

The application failed to properly handle the scenario where the 'read operations index' (READ_OPERATIONS_INDEX) could not be loaded during startup. Instead of terminating, it would log a warning and proceed with READ_OPERATIONS_INDEX set to None, leading to a bypass of security policy checks for AWS API calls.

### Impact

An attacker could bypass the intended security policy, allowing the execution of operations that should have been denied or required consent, potentially leading to unauthorized actions within the AWS environment.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/aws-api-mcp-server/awslabs/aws_api_mcp_server/server.py
+++ b/src/aws-api-mcp-server/awslabs/aws_api_mcp_server/server.py
@@ -319,26 +317,23 @@ async def call_aws_helper(
     )
 
     try:
-        # Check security policy
-        if READ_OPERATIONS_INDEX is not None:
-            policy_decision = check_security_policy(ir, READ_OPERATIONS_INDEX, ctx)
-            if policy_decision == PolicyDecision.DENY:
-                error_message = 'Execution of this operation is denied by security policy.'
-                await ctx.error(error_message)
-                raise AwsApiMcpError(error_message)
-            elif policy_decision == PolicyDecision.ELICIT:
-                await request_consent(cli_command, ctx)
-        else:
-            if READ_OPERATIONS_ONLY_MODE:
-                error_message = (
-                    'Execution of this operation is not allowed because read only mode is enabled. '
-                    f'It can be disabled by setting the {READ_ONLY_KEY} environment variable to False.'
-                )
-                await ctx.error(error_message)
-                raise AwsApiMcpError(error_message)
-            elif REQUIRE_MUTATION_CONSENT:
-                await request_consent(cli_command, ctx)
+        # Check security policy.
+        if READ_OPERATIONS_INDEX is None:
+            error_message = (
+                'Execution of this operation is denied because the security policy '
+                'enforcement data failed to initialize.'
+            )
+            await ctx.error(error_message)
+            raise AwsApiMcpError(error_message)
+
+        policy_decision = check_security_policy(ir, READ_OPERATIONS_INDEX, ctx)
+
+        if policy_decision == PolicyDecision.DENY:
+            error_message = 'Execution of this operation is denied by security policy.'
+            await ctx.error(error_message)
+            raise AwsApiMcpError(error_message)
+        elif policy_decision == PolicyDecision.ELICIT:
+            await request_consent(cli_command, ctx)
 
         if ir.command and ir.command.is_help_operation:
             return await get_help_document(cli_command, ctx)
@@ -437,9 +432,13 @@ def main():
     try:
         READ_OPERATIONS_INDEX = get_read_only_operations()
     except Exception as e:
-        logger.warning('Failed to load read operations index: {}', e)
-        READ_OPERATIONS_INDEX = None
+        logger.error(
+            'Failed to load read operations index required for security policy '
+            'enforcement; refusing to start: {}',
+            e,
+        )
+        raise
 
     if TRANSPORT == 'stdio':
         server.run(
```

### Fix summary

The patch modifies the startup logic to ensure that if the 'read operations index' fails to load, the server will refuse to start by raising an exception. It also explicitly checks if READ_OPERATIONS_INDEX is None before any policy checks and denies execution if it is, preventing policy bypasses.

## GHSA-4jwf-m4wg-8p66 · Microsoft.OpenApi.Kiota.Builder

2026-07-24 · C# · **CRITICAL** 0.0 · Pattern: `PATH_TRAVERSAL→FILE_READ`

### Root cause

The application was directly using user-controlled input from the `static_template` field within `x-ai-*` extensions without proper validation. This allowed an attacker to specify arbitrary file paths or URLs, which would then be included in the generated Copilot plugin manifest.

### Impact

An attacker could inject arbitrary file paths or URLs into the generated plugin manifest, potentially leading to local file inclusion (reading sensitive files) or remote resource loading (SSRF) when the plugin is consumed by a Copilot instance. This could also lead to information disclosure or arbitrary code execution depending on the context of the loaded resource.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/Kiota.Builder/OpenApiExtensions/OpenApiAiCapabilitiesExtension.cs
+++ b/src/Kiota.Builder/OpenApiExtensions/OpenApiAiCapabilitiesExtension.cs
@@ -146,7 +146,7 @@ private static ExtensionResponseSemantics ParseResponseSemantics(JsonObject sour
         if (source.TryGetPropertyValue(nameof(ResponseSemantics.StaticTemplate).ToFirstCharacterLowerCase().ToSnakeCase(), out var staticTemplate) &&
             staticTemplate is JsonObject staticTemplateObj)
         {
-            responseSemantics.StaticTemplate = staticTemplateObj;
+            responseSemantics.StaticTemplate = new ExtensionResponseSemanticsStaticTemplate { Template = staticTemplateObj };
         }
 
         if (source.TryGetPropertyValue(nameof(ResponseSemantics.Properties).ToFirstCharacterLowerCase(), out var properties) &&
@@ -354,7 +354,7 @@ public bool? IsNonConsequential
 public class ExtensionResponseSemantics
 {
     public string DataPath { get; set; } = string.Empty;
-    public object? StaticTemplate
+    public ExtensionResponseSemanticsStaticTemplate? StaticTemplate
     {
         get; set;
     }
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a new class `ExtensionResponseSemanticsStaticTemplate` to encapsulate the `static_template` content. This class includes validation logic (`IsSafeFileReference`) to prevent absolute URIs, rooted paths, Windows drive paths, and parent-directory traversals ('..') in file references. The patch also updates the parsing and writing logic to use this new class and logs warnings for unsafe file references.

## GHSA-6vch-q96h-7gc3 · go.etcd.io/etcd/v3

2026-07-24 · Go · **HIGH** 0.0 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The `tlsListener.acceptLoop` function spawned a new goroutine for each incoming connection to perform a TLS handshake. However, there was no deadline set for these handshakes, allowing a malicious client to initiate many connections and then stall the handshake process indefinitely. This led to an unbounded creation of goroutines.

### Impact

An attacker could exhaust system resources (CPU, memory) by initiating a large number of stalled TLS handshakes, leading to a denial of service for legitimate clients attempting to connect to the etcd server.

### Key diff

```
--- a/client/pkg/transport/listener_tls.go
+++ b/client/pkg/transport/listener_tls.go
@@ -143,6 +149,7 @@ func (l *tlsListener) acceptLoop() {
 
 			tlsConn := conn.(*tls.Conn)
+			_ = tlsConn.SetDeadline(time.Now().Add(tlsHandshakeTimeout))
 			herr := tlsConn.Handshake()
 			pendingMu.Lock()
 			delete(pending, conn)
@@ -152,6 +160,7 @@ func (l *tlsListener) acceptLoop() {
 				l.handshakeFailure(tlsConn, herr)
 				return
 			}
+			_ = tlsConn.SetDeadline(time.Time{})
 			if err := l.check(ctx, tlsConn); err != nil {
 				l.handshakeFailure(tlsConn, err)
 				return
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a `tlsHandshakeTimeout` constant and applies it as a deadline to the `tls.Conn` object before the `Handshake()` call. After the handshake completes (successfully or not), the deadline is reset to prevent it from affecting subsequent operations on the connection.

## GHSA-chx6-hx7r-mcp5 · react-router

2026-07-24 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 0.0 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from inefficient route matching logic in React Router. When processing manifest requests, especially those with a 'paths' query parameter, the server-side code would generate all ancestor paths for each requested path. This process involved string splitting and joining in a loop, leading to a significant performance degradation and resource exhaustion when a large number of paths or very long paths were provided in the URL.

### Impact

An unauthenticated attacker could craft a malicious URL with a long 'paths' query parameter, causing the server to perform excessive computations. This would lead to a denial of service, making the application unresponsive or crashing the server.

### Key diff

```
--- a/packages/react-router/lib/rsc/server.rsc.ts
+++ b/packages/react-router/lib/rsc/server.rsc.ts
@@ -532,6 +533,14 @@ async function generateManifestResponse(
   temporaryReferences: unknown,
   routeDiscovery: RouteDiscovery | undefined,
 ) {
+  let url = new URL(request.url);
+  if (url.toString().length > URL_LIMIT) {
+    return new Response(null, {
+      statusText: "Bad Request",
+      status: 400,
+    });
+  }
+
   if (routeDiscovery?.mode === "initial") {
     let payload: RSCManifestPayload = {
       type: "manifest",
@@ -550,7 +559,6 @@ async function generateManifestResponse(
     );
   }
 
-  let url = new URL(request.url);
   let pathParam = url.searchParams.get("paths");
   let pathnames = pathParam
     ? pathParam.split(",").filter(Boolean)

--- a/packages/react-router/lib/rsc/server.rsc.ts
+++ b/packages/react-router/lib/rsc/server.rsc.ts
@@ -1428,33 +1436,18 @@ async function getAdditionalRoutePatches(
   let matchedPaths = new Set<string>();
 
   for (const pathname of pathnames) {
-    let segments = pathname.split("/").filter(Boolean);
-    let paths: string[] = ["/"];
-
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a `URL_LIMIT` to immediately reject overly long URLs, preventing the expensive processing. It also refactors the `getAdditionalRoutePatches` and `handleManifestRequest` functions to use a new `getPathsWithAncestors` utility. This utility efficiently generates ancestor paths and ensures that duplicate paths are not processed multiple times, significantly reducing the computational overhead.

## GHSA-g357-x5c3-c72p · liquidjs

2026-07-24 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 0.0 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The `pop` filter in LiquidJS did not account for the memory usage of the array it processed, unlike other array-manipulating filters. This oversight allowed an attacker to craft an input that would consume excessive memory without being limited by the `memoryLimit` configuration.

### Impact

An attacker could provide a large array to the `pop` filter, leading to uncontrolled memory consumption and potentially a denial of service for the application hosting the LiquidJS template engine.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/filters/array.ts
+++ b/src/filters/array.ts
@@ -88,8 +88,10 @@ export function unshift<T> (this: FilterImpl, v: T[], arg: T): T[] {
   return clone
 }
 
-export function pop<T> (v: T[]): T[] {
-  const clone = [...toArray(v)]
+export function pop<T> (this: FilterImpl, v: T[]): T[] {
+  const array = toArray(v)
+  this.context.memoryLimit.use(array.length)
+  const clone = [...array]
   clone.pop()
   return clone
 }
```

### Fix summary

The patch adds a call to `this.context.memoryLimit.use(array.length)` within the `pop` filter, ensuring that the memory usage of the input array is properly accounted for and constrained by the configured `memoryLimit`. A similar fix was applied to the `sample` filter to use `v.length` instead of `count` for memory accounting.

## GHSA-gf8h-gq53-288j · org.openidentityplatform.openam:openam-auth-webauthn

2026-07-24 · Java · **CRITICAL** 0.0 · Pattern: `DESERIALIZATION→RCE`

### Root cause

The application used Java deserialization of user-controlled data (Authenticator objects) without sufficiently strict validation of the object graph. The original ObjectInputFilter only checked the class at depth 1, allowing an attacker to bypass the filter by embedding malicious gadget classes at deeper levels within the deserialized object graph.

### Impact

An attacker could achieve Remote Code Execution (RCE) by crafting a malicious serialized object that, when deserialized, would execute arbitrary code on the server.

### Key diff

```
-				ois.setObjectInputFilter(filterInfo -> {
-					if(filterInfo.depth() > 1) {
-						return ObjectInputFilter.Status.ALLOWED;
-					}
-					Class<?> cls = filterInfo.serialClass();
-					if (ALLOWED_AUTHENTICATOR_CLASSES.contains(cls.getName())) {
+				ois.setObjectInputFilter(filterInfo -> {
+					Class<?> cls = filterInfo.serialClass();
+					if (filterInfo.depth() == 1 && !ALLOWED_AUTHENTICATOR_CLASSES.contains(className)) {
+						return ObjectInputFilter.Status.REJECTED;
+					}
+					if (className.startsWith(WEBAUTHN4J_PACKAGE_PREFIX)
+							|| ALLOWED_JDK_CLASSES.contains(className)) {
```

### Fix summary

The patch significantly enhanced the ObjectInputFilter by implementing a more granular allowlist. It now explicitly allows only specific classes from the webauthn4j package and a predefined set of safe JDK classes, and it applies these checks recursively to all depths of the object graph, not just the root object.

## GHSA-hp6v-6jw7-gv2f · @budibase/server

2026-07-24 · JavaScript · **CRITICAL** 0.0 · Pattern: `MISSING_VERIFICATION→SIGNATURE`

### Root cause

The OIDC SSO implementation in Budibase linked incoming identity provider accounts to existing local accounts solely based on the email address provided by the IdP. Crucially, it did not verify if the email address itself was marked as 'verified' by the IdP, allowing an attacker to claim an unverified email and link it to a victim's account.

### Impact

An attacker could create an OIDC account with an unverified email matching a victim's Budibase account. Upon logging in via SSO, the attacker's unverified OIDC identity would be linked to the victim's Budibase account, granting the attacker full control over the victim's account.

### Key diff

```
-	  if (!dbUser) {
+	  if (!dbUser && (details.emailVerified || allowUnverifiedEmailLinking)) {
     dbUser = await users.getGlobalUserByEmail(details.email)
   }
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a check for `emailVerified` status from the OIDC provider. Account linking by email now only proceeds if the email is explicitly marked as verified by the IdP, or if a new `allowUnverifiedEmailLinking` configuration option is explicitly enabled (defaulting to false). This prevents unverified emails from being used to link accounts.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 40 occurrences**

## GHSA-hq9q-27g5-qwpj · Microsoft.OpenApi.Kiota.Builder

2026-07-24 · C# · **CRITICAL** 0.0 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→COMMAND`

### Root cause

The application was vulnerable because it directly used the `DependencyInstallCommand` property from the `x-ms-kiota-info` OpenAPI extension without proper sanitization or validation. This property, intended to specify a command for installing dependencies, was parsed from external OpenAPI descriptions, allowing an attacker to inject arbitrary shell commands.

### Impact

An attacker could achieve arbitrary command execution on the system running the `kiota info` command. This could lead to full system compromise, data exfiltration, or further network penetration.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/Kiota.Builder/LanguageInformation.cs
+++ b/src/Kiota.Builder/LanguageInformation.cs
@@ -40,10 +40,6 @@ public void SerializeInternal(IOpenApiWriter writer, Action<IOpenApiWriter, Lang
         writer.WriteStartObject();
         writer.WriteProperty(nameof(MaturityLevel).ToFirstCharacterLowerCase(), MaturityLevel.ToString());
         writer.WriteProperty(nameof(SupportExperience).ToFirstCharacterLowerCase(), SupportExperience.ToString());
-        writer.WriteProperty(nameof(DependencyInstallCommand).ToFirstCharacterLowerCase(), DependencyInstallCommand);
         writer.WriteOptionalCollection(nameof(Dependencies).ToFirstCharacterLowerCase(), Dependencies, callback);
         writer.WriteProperty(nameof(ClientClassName).ToFirstCharacterLowerCase(), ClientClassName);
         writer.WriteProperty(nameof(ClientNamespaceName).ToFirstCharacterLowerCase(), ClientNamespaceName);
@@ -59,10 +58,6 @@ public static LanguageInformation Parse(JsonNode source)
                 if (entry is not null)
                     extension.Dependencies.Add(LanguageDependency.Parse(entry));
         }
-        if (rawObject.TryGetPropertyValue(nameof(DependencyInstallCommand).ToFirstCharacterLowerCase(), out var installCommand) && installCommand is JsonValue stringValue)
-        {
-            extension.DependencyInstallCommand = stringValue.GetValue<string>();
-        }
         // not parsing the maturity level on purpose, we don't want APIs to be able to change that
         if (rawObject.TryGetPropertyValue(nameof(ClientClassName).ToFirstCharacterLowerCase(), out var clientClassName) && clientClassName is JsonValue clientClassNameValue)
```

### Fix summary

The patch removes the parsing and serialization of the `DependencyInstallCommand` property from the `LanguageInformation` class and the `OpenApiKiotaExtension`. This eliminates the vulnerable code path that allowed external OpenAPI descriptions to dictate system commands.

## GHSA-p2f4-r6v6-j797 · builder-util-runtime

2026-07-24 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 0.0 · Pattern: `CREDENTIAL_LEAK→LOG_EXPOSURE`

### Root cause

The application was not properly stripping sensitive HTTP headers like 'Authorization' and 'PRIVATE-TOKEN' during cross-origin redirects. Additionally, debug logs and error messages could expose sensitive data like tokens and passwords because the `safeStringifyJson` function had an incomplete list of sensitive field names and did not properly redact them.

### Impact

An attacker could potentially intercept sensitive credentials (e.g., `PRIVATE-TOKEN`, `Authorization` headers) if a user's client was redirected to a malicious or compromised third-party domain. Debug logs and error messages could also leak credentials, aiding an attacker in gaining unauthorized access.

### Key diff

```
--- a/packages/builder-util-runtime/src/httpExecutor.ts
+++ b/packages/builder-util-runtime/src/httpExecutor.ts
@@ -320,17 +357,21 @@ Please double check that your authentication token is correct. Due to security r
   static prepareRedirectUrlOptions(redirectUrl: string, options: RequestOptions): RequestOptions {
     const newOptions = configureRequestOptionsFromUrl(redirectUrl, { ...options })
     const headers = newOptions.headers
-    if (headers?.authorization) {
-      // Parse original and redirect URLs to compare origins
-      const originalUrl = HttpExecutor.reconstructOriginalUrl(options)
-      const parsedRedirectUrl = parseUrl(redirectUrl, options)
-
-      // Strip authorization header on cross-origin redirects (different protocol, hostname, or port)
-      if (HttpExecutor.isCrossOriginRedirect(originalUrl, parsedRedirectUrl)) {
-        if (debug.enabled) {
-          debug(`Given the cross-origin redirect (from ${originalUrl.host} to ${parsedRedirectUrl.host}), the Authorization header will be stripped out.`)
+    if (headers == null) {
+      return newOptions
+    }
+
+    const originalUrl = HttpExecutor.reconstructOriginalUrl(options)
+    const parsedRedirectUrl = parseUrl(redirectUrl, options)
+
+    if (HttpExecutor.isCrossOriginRedirect(originalUrl, parsedRedirectUrl)) {
+      if (debug.enabled) {
+        debug(`Cross-origin redirect (${originalUrl.host} → ${parsedRedirectUrl.host}): stripping sensitive headers`)
+      }
+      for (const key of Object.keys(headers)) {
+        if (SENSITIVE_REDIRECT_HEADERS.has(normalizeName(key))) {
+          delete (headers as Record<string, unknown>)[key]
         }
-        delete headers.authorization
       }
     }
     return newOptions
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a comprehensive registry of sensitive HTTP headers to be stripped on cross-origin redirects and a more robust mechanism for identifying and redacting sensitive field names in JSON data for logging. It also allows for dynamic registration of additional sensitive headers and field patterns.

> → GHSA-pm7q-rjjx-979p
> → GHSA-f7j3-774f-rfhj
> → GHSA-vh4v-2xq2-g5cg
> → GHSA-q62h-354g-5r85
> → GHSA-pf56-329r-95rw

## GHSA-w4hw-qcx7-56pr · shescape

2026-07-24 · JavaScript · **CRITICAL** 0.0 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→COMMAND`

### Root cause

The `shescape` library, specifically its Windows CMD escape function, failed to properly escape parentheses `()` characters. This allowed an attacker to inject arbitrary commands when user-controlled input containing these characters was passed to a shell command.

### Impact

An attacker could execute arbitrary commands on the system where the vulnerable application is running, leading to full system compromise.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/internal/win/cmd.js
+++ b/src/internal/win/cmd.js
@@ -14,7 +14,7 @@ import RegExp from "../regexp.cjs";
 export function getEscapeFunction() {
   const controls = new RegExp(/[ ]/g);
   const newlines = new RegExp(/\n/g);
-  const specials = new RegExp(/([%&<>^|])/g);
+  const specials = new RegExp(/([%&()<>^|])/g);
   const quotes = new RegExp(/"/g);
```

### Fix summary

The patch updates the regular expression in the Windows CMD escape function to include parentheses `()` as special characters that need to be escaped. Additionally, the `compose` function logic was refactored to correctly handle fragments and ensure proper escaping of the initial part of a command.

## GHSA-xg4h-6gfc-h4m8 · go.etcd.io/etcd/v3

2026-07-24 · Go · **HIGH** 0.0 · Pattern: `MISSING_AUTHZ→RESOURCE`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from an incorrect handling of watch requests with open-ended ranges. Specifically, the authorization logic for watch requests did not correctly interpret or apply permissions when a client requested to watch a key range that was effectively 'all keys' (e.g., by specifying a key of '\x00' and an empty or null RangeEnd). This allowed an attacker to bypass granular authorization checks.

### Impact

An attacker could bypass authorization rules and gain unauthorized access to watch any key-value pair stored in etcd, regardless of the permissions configured for their role. This leads to unauthorized information disclosure.

### Key diff

```
--- a/server/etcdserver/api/v3rpc/watch.go
+++ b/server/etcdserver/api/v3rpc/watch.go
@@ -260,15 +260,6 @@ func (sws *serverWatchStream) recvLoop() error {
 				// \x00 is the smallest key
 				creq.Key = []byte{0}
 			}
-			if len(creq.RangeEnd) == 0 {
-				// force nil since watchstream.Watch distinguishes
-				// between nil and []byte{} for single key / >=
-				creq.RangeEnd = nil
-			}
-			if len(creq.RangeEnd) == 1 && creq.RangeEnd[0] == 0 {
-				// support  >= key queries
-				creq.RangeEnd = []byte{}
-			}
 			if creq.StartRevision < 0 {
 				wr := &pb.WatchResponse{
 					Header:       sws.newResponseHeader(sws.watchStream.Rev()),
@@ -319,6 +310,16 @@ func (sws *serverWatchStream) recvLoop() error {
 				}
 			}
 
+			if len(creq.RangeEnd) == 0 {
+				// force nil since watchstream.Watch distinguishes
+				// between nil and []byte{} for single key / >=
+				creq.RangeEnd = nil
+			}
+			if len(creq.RangeEnd) == 1 && creq.RangeEnd[0] == 0 {
+				// support  >= key queries
+				creq.RangeEnd = []byte{}
+			}
+
 			filters := FiltersFromRequest(creq)
 
 			id, err := sws.watchStream.Watch(mvcc.WatchID(creq.WatchId), creq.Key, creq.RangeEnd, creq.StartRevision, filters...)
```

### Fix summary

The patch moves the logic that normalizes `RangeEnd` values (converting empty or `\x00` to `nil` or `[]byte{}`) to after the authorization check. This ensures that the authorization logic correctly processes the original, potentially open-ended range request before it is normalized, thereby enforcing proper access controls.
